They’re doing all that water main construction on 4th street, so they blocked off the main garage, which makes zero sense because it's the busy season and the detour signs are practically invisible. I got a parking ticket near there once just for being maybe two inches into the red zone, and the meter maid was literally standing there waiting for the clock to tick over. \n\nBut... yeah, lately I just leave my painting supplies completely untouched on my desk. I even bought these really nice new sable brushes right before I started feeling this way. Real sable, which is getting so hard to find because everyone uses the synthetic stuff now. Which I get, you know, ethical reasons and all that, but the water retention on the synthetic ones just isn't the same at all if you're trying to do a proper watercolor wash. But anyway... I just can’t make myself care enough to go to the museum or even pick up a brush right now. It just sits there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. A lot, actually. I just keep replaying every missed family plan in my head. Like last weekend, we were supposed to go to my mother-in-law's for her birthday, and I was supposed to pick up the cake from that bakery on Elm. The one that makes the really good Italian buttercream? Not that overly sweet fondant stuff. I really don't understand the obsession with fondant these days, it literally tastes like sweet marshmallow plastic. You watch those baking shows and the judges just peel it right off anyway before they eat it. My daughter watches those baking competitions constantly, the ones where they only have an hour and inevitably drop a tier of the cake on the floor. \n\nBut anyway... I just couldn't get it together to go get the cake, and my husband had to scramble to find a grocery store one, and we had this really tense conversation about it. I keep replaying that, and every other tense conversation we've had lately. I just feel like I’m letting everyone down right now. As a wife, as a mother... even as a manager at work. I just feel like a disappointment across the board.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: No, no. I mean, I feel terrible, but I don't want to die or anything like that. I just... I wish I could just hit pause for a while, you know? Just go somewhere quiet where no one needs anything from me. My sister actually went to this silent retreat up in the mountains last year. Ten whole days where you aren't allowed to speak, no phones, no reading, nothing. They just sit there and meditate and eat lentils or whatever. I don't think I could do the food, honestly. Lentils always give me terrible heartburn. I tried making this lentil soup once from a recipe I found on Pinterest, and it called for smoked paprika, which I usually love, but it just completely overpowered the whole dish. My husband wouldn't even eat it, and he'll usually eat anything. He grew up with four brothers, so at his house, if you didn't eat fast, you didn't eat at all. But anyway, no, I don't want to disappear permanently. I'm just so tired.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about a month since things really shifted. Before that, I was actually feeling fantastic, like I had all this energy and drive. I even reorganized my entire department at work, which honestly needed doing for years. The filing system was completely archaic—like, who still uses color-coded manila folders for digital assets? My old boss, Richard, insisted on it, and he was one of those guys who printed out his emails just to read them. He retired to Florida last year, somewhere near Naples, I think. Or maybe Sarasota? My parents used to have a timeshare in Sarasota right on the water, but the red tide got so bad they had to sell it. The smell was just unbelievable, like rotting fish baking in the sun. You couldn't even sit on the balcony without your eyes watering. But anyway... yeah, this low feeling, this total lack of energy after being so driven, it's been going on for about a month now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of worry or fear that you can't control?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. I mean, I'm not sitting around worrying about things happening, it's just more this heaviness, you know? Like I just can't get moving. But no real fear or panic. My dog, though, she's been terrified of everything lately. We have this Golden Retriever mix, Bailey, and she used to be so brave, but ever since that huge thunderstorm we had back in July, she's been a wreck. The thunder was so loud it actually shook the pictures on the walls. We have this one framed poster from when my husband and I went to the jazz festival in Montreal, and it fell right off the hook and the glass shattered everywhere. Montreal is such a beautiful city in the summer, the food is incredible. We had these bagels there that are totally different from New York bagels, they boil them in honey water or something? Anyway, Bailey just shakes whenever the wind blows now. But me? No, no uncontrollable worry. Just... just so heavy and tired.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, usually I love painting, and I'm always the first one to want to go wandering through new museum exhibits. But recently... I just leave my supplies sitting there completely untouched, and I just can't make myself care enough to go anywhere. \n\nThere was actually this big modern art exhibit that opened downtown last week, and my friend Sarah got tickets for us months ago. Sarah is always so organized with things like that, she has this physical planner she carries everywhere with all these little stickers. I don't know how she keeps up with it all, honestly. She has three kids and they're all in travel soccer. Do you know how much driving travel soccer involves? They were in Ohio last weekend, and the weekend before that they had to drive all the way up to Pennsylvania. I get tired just hearing about it. I drive a Honda CRV and the seats are usually really comfortable for road trips—we took it to Maine a few years ago and it was great—but right now even just driving to the grocery store feels like a massive chore. \n\nBut anyway... yeah, I ended up telling Sarah I couldn't go to the museum. I just don't have any interest in my art or going out to see it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I haven't really been eating much. I've just been picking at my meals, or honestly, I'll completely forget to eat lunch at work. It's funny because my office is right next to this amazing little deli. They make these incredible turkey and cranberry sandwiches on sourdough. The sourdough comes from this bakery across town that's been run by the same family for like, three generations. The grandfather still comes in every morning at 4 AM to start the dough, which is just wild to me. I can't imagine getting up that early every single day. I used to be an early riser when I was in college, I'd get up and go for a run before my 8 AM classes. I went to school in upstate New York, so running in the winter was brutal. The snow would get so deep, sometimes they wouldn't even plow the side streets, and you'd just be trudging through it. My roommate, Jessica, she was from California and had never even seen snow before we moved in together. She bought this massive, puffy coat that made her look like a marshmallow, but she still froze. We lost touch after graduation, which is a shame. I think she moved to Chicago? Or maybe Seattle. But anyway, yeah, I've noticed my skirts are fitting a little looser lately, which is weird because I'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything. I just... I don't really have the appetite for it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your sleep pattern over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been really messed up lately. Even when I make a point to get into bed at a reasonable hour, I just... I wake up around four or five in the morning, and my brain just starts going. I start thinking about work, and things with my husband, and then I'm just done sleeping. I can't get back to sleep at all. \n\nIt's actually really frustrating because our mattress is supposed to be one of those super comfortable memory foam ones. We bought it last year during a Presidents' Day sale. You know how those furniture stores always have those crazy holiday sales with the giant inflatable gorillas out front? There's this one store on the highway near our house that has this guy who stands out there spinning a sign, no matter what the weather is. I always feel so bad for him when it's raining. I mean, they must pay him decently, right? Or maybe it's just a high school kid looking for some extra cash. My nephew, Ryan, just started his first job at a movie theater. He gets free popcorn, which sounds amazing, but he says the smell of butter gets stuck in his clothes and he can't wash it out. He's saving up for a car. He really wants a Jeep, but my sister is terrified of him driving one because they can roll over. She's always been a bit of a worrier, ever since we were kids. \n\nBut yeah, I just... I lie there at 4 AM, wide awake, and I just can't turn my brain off to go back to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Well, actually, yeah. My staff at work has definitely noticed it. I'm a manager, so I run a lot of meetings, and lately they've been pointing out that I'm just... slower to answer questions. Like there's this long pause before I can even get my thoughts together and get the words out. \n\nIt's really embarrassing, honestly, especially in front of the newer hires. We just brought on this young guy, Michael, straight out of college. He went to Penn State, which is where my cousin's daughter is looking to go next year. She wants to study marine biology, which I think is just fascinating. I took a marine biology elective once and we got to go on this field trip to an aquarium and actually touch the stingrays. They feel so weird, kind of slimy but smooth at the same time. The aquarium was down in Baltimore, right on the Inner Harbor. Have you ever been there? They have these amazing crab shacks right on the water. My husband and I went to one for our anniversary a few years ago and we got so messy cracking the shells, they had to give us these giant plastic bibs with cartoon crabs on them. I think I still have a picture of him wearing it somewhere on my phone. He hates seafood now, though, ever since he got food poisoning from some bad oysters at a work conference in Las Vegas. Vegas is just too much for me, honestly. All the flashing lights and the noise, it gives me a headache. \n\nBut anyway, yeah... the meetings. It's just taking me longer to process things and respond to people. Like I'm wading through molasses just to speak.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things would be better for the people around you if you weren't there?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I haven't really felt that way. I mean, I know I'm a bit slower and more tired lately, but I don't think anyone would be better off without me. \n\nThough, speaking of being better off, my sister just adopted this rescue dog, a golden retriever mix named Barnaby, and she says her whole life is better now. He's got this goofy smile and one floppy ear that never stands up straight. The rescue organization she got him from is actually run by this retired couple who converted their entire farmhouse into a dog sanctuary. They have like, twenty dogs running around at any given time. It sounds like chaos, but my sister said it was the most peaceful place. It's out in the country, past all those old apple orchards. We used to go apple picking out there every fall when we were kids. My mom would make these massive batches of homemade applesauce and can them in those glass Mason jars. She had this whole system with boiling water and tongs, and she'd always make us label them with these little sticky tags she bought at the craft store. The craft store closed down a few years ago, which is sad. It got replaced by one of those giant chain pharmacies. It seems like there's a pharmacy on every corner now, doesn't it? \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't... I don't feel like I'm a burden or that they'd be better off without me. Just feeling a little off my game, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death recurring in your mind, even if you don't want to die?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really been thinking about death. I mean, my brain is definitely spinning a lot in the early mornings, but it's not about that. \n\nAlthough, my husband has been watching this true crime documentary series on Netflix lately, and it's all about these cold cases from the seventies. It's so creepy. I don't know how he can watch that stuff right before bed. We actually had to upgrade our Netflix subscription because he wanted to watch it in 4K. He bought this massive new TV for the living room right before the Super Bowl. It's so big, we had to rearrange all the furniture just to make it fit. We ended up moving my favorite reading chair into the guest room, which is fine, I guess, because the lighting in there is actually really good in the afternoons. The guest room gets the western sun, so it warms up nicely. I have this beautiful spider plant hanging in the window in there, and it's just thriving. I tried growing succulents a few years ago because everyone said they were impossible to kill, but I somehow managed to drown them. I guess I overwatered them. My mother always had a green thumb, she could make anything grow. She had this huge vegetable garden in our backyard when I was growing up, and we'd eat fresh tomatoes all summer long. \n\nBut yeah, no... no thoughts about death or anything like that. Just the usual stress keeping me up.\n\nDoctor: Has your current state of feeling heavy and tired been consistent over the past month, or have you had any periods where you felt somewhat better?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been pretty consistent. Like, for about a month now, I've just been in this... this low place. Which is so weird because right before this, I was so energized and driven, getting everything done at work and at home. \n\nSpeaking of getting things done, I finally got around to organizing the garage last month when I had all that energy. We had so many boxes just sitting there since we moved in five years ago. My husband is a bit of a packrat, he keeps everything. He still has his old baseball cards from when he was a kid. He keeps them in these special plastic sleeves in a binder. I think he thinks they're going to be worth a fortune someday, but I looked up a few of them on eBay once and they were selling for like, two dollars. I buy a lot of stuff on eBay, actually. Mostly vintage Pyrex bowls. I love the primary colors, they just look so cheerful in the kitchen. I have this yellow mixing bowl that I use for everything, especially when I make my grandmother's banana bread recipe. She always used overripe bananas, the ones that are almost completely black. She said that's the secret to making it really sweet. She used to bake all the time, she made these incredible snickerdoodles for the church bake sale every year. The church we went to had this gorgeous stained glass window with a dove in the center. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the tiredness. It's just been a solid month of feeling really low and heavy, especially after having all that drive right before.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood stays with you all day, or are there times when you feel a little better?\n\nPatient: Well, it's pretty much there all day. Most days lately, I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like I'm already disappointed in the day before it even starts. It just kind of sits on me. \n\nWhich is frustrating because I used to love mornings. I used to get up early and make coffee in this French press I bought at a little boutique downtown. It's one of those shiny copper ones, really pretty. The shop I got it from is right next to this amazing bakery that makes the best almond croissants. They're huge, like the size of your hand, and covered in powdered sugar. I always end up getting it all over my clothes when I eat them. I have to carry a Tide pen in my purse now just in case. My purse is actually getting ridiculous, it's so heavy. I have like three different shades of lipstick in there, my planner, my wallet, and all these receipts I keep meaning to scan for work. I'm a manager, so I have to track all my expenses, and the new software they made us start using is just a nightmare. It takes ten steps just to upload one receipt. The IT guy, Kevin, tried to walk me through it on Zoom, but his microphone kept cutting out, so half the time he sounded like a robot. He has this huge collection of vintage action figures on the shelves behind his desk. I think they were Star Wars, but I couldn't really tell. My brother was obsessed with Star Wars when we were kids. He had the Millennium Falcon toy and he'd run around the house making the spaceship noises until my dad told him to knock it off. \n\nBut yeah, the low mood doesn't really lift. It's just this constant, heavy gray feeling from the minute I wake up until I go to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Is your low mood worse at any particular time of day?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed it being worse at a specific time, to be honest. It's just kind of... there. Always. \n\nAlthough, speaking of time of day, I've noticed the mail carrier has been coming a lot later in the afternoon recently. Usually, he's here by noon, but lately it's been closer to four o'clock. We have this really nice mail carrier, Frank. He always leaves a Milk-Bone for our neighbor's golden retriever. That dog is so sweet, his name is Buster. Buster actually got out of their yard last week and I found him wandering near the storm drain at the end of the street. It was right after that huge thunderstorm we had on Tuesday. The thunder was so loud it rattled the windows in the living room. We have these original single-pane windows from when the house was built in the fifties, and they are terrible for insulation. Our heating bill in the winter is just outrageous. I keep telling my husband we need to get them replaced, but he says it's not in the budget right now because of the new TV. He loves that TV. He was watching a documentary about the history of jazz the other night, and the sound quality was actually really impressive. I don't know much about jazz, but my grandfather used to play the saxophone. He had this beautiful brass saxophone that he kept polished to a shine. He played in a little band on the weekends at this local Italian restaurant. The restaurant made the most amazing garlic knots, they were always drowning in butter and parsley. \n\nBut no, I don't think the mood is worse at any particular time. It's just a steady heaviness all day long.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood can be lifted or made worse by things that happen during the day, like conversations with your family or events at work?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. It doesn't seem to matter what's going on around me. Like, even if I have a good conversation, the heaviness doesn't budge. \n\nActually, my sister called me just yesterday while I was on my lunch break. She's planning this huge family trip to Florida for next spring. She wants us all to rent this massive house near Disney World. I haven't been to Disney since I was a teenager. I remember it being so hot, just incredibly humid. We rode that Pirates of the Caribbean ride like four times just to sit in the air conditioning. My dad bought these ridiculous matching neon green t-shirts for all of us so we wouldn't get lost in the crowds. He was always so paranoid about losing one of us. He used to make us hold hands whenever we crossed the street, even when we were way too old for it. He was a safety inspector for the city, so I guess he just couldn't turn it off. He always had a hard hat and a clipboard in the back of his truck. It was this ancient Ford, manual transmission. I actually learned how to drive stick on that thing. Stalled it right in the middle of a busy intersection on my very first lesson, people were honking and I was just sweating through my shirt... \n\nBut, um, what were we saying? Oh, right. Yeah, even when she was telling me all about the vacation plans and getting all excited, I just couldn't feel it. Nothing really lifts it or makes it worse, it just stays exactly the same.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. We just brought on this young guy, Michael, straight out of college. He went to Penn State, which is where my cousin's daughter is looking to go next year. She wants to study marine biology, which I think is just fascinating. I took a marine biology elective once and we got to go on this field trip to an aquarium and actually touch the stingrays. They feel so weird, kind of slimy but smooth at the same time. The aquarium was down in Baltimore, right on the Inner Harbor. Have you ever been there? They have these amazing crab shacks right on the water. My husband and I went to one for our anniversary a few years ago and we got so messy cracking the shells, they had to give us these giant plastic bibs with cartoon crabs on them. I think I still have a picture of him wearing it somewhere on my phone. He hates seafood now, though, ever since he got food poisoning from some bad oysters at a work conference in Las Vegas. Vegas is just too much for me, honestly. All the flashing lights and the noise, it gives me a headache. \n\nBut anyway, yeah... the meetings. It's just taking me longer to process things and respond to people. Like I'm wading through molasses just to speak.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things would be better for the people around you if you weren't there?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I haven't really felt that way. I mean, I know I'm a bit slower and more tired lately, but I don't think anyone would be better off without me. \n\nThough, speaking of being better off, my sister just adopted this rescue dog, a golden retriever mix named Barnaby, and she says her whole life is better now. He's got this goofy smile and one floppy ear that never stands up straight. The rescue organization she got him from is actually run by this retired couple who converted their entire farmhouse into a dog sanctuary. They have like, twenty dogs running around at any given time. It sounds like chaos, but my sister said it was the most peaceful place. It's out in the country, past all those old apple orchards. We used to go apple picking out there every fall when we were kids. My mom would make these massive batches of homemade applesauce and can them in those glass Mason jars. She had this whole system with boiling water and tongs, and she'd always make us label them with these little sticky tags she bought at the craft store. The craft store closed down a few years ago, which is sad. It got replaced by one of those giant chain pharmacies. It seems like there's a pharmacy on every corner now, doesn't it? \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't... I don't feel like I'm a burden or that they'd be better off without me. Just feeling a little off my game, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death recurring in your mind, even if you don't want to die?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really been thinking about death. I mean, my brain is definitely spinning a lot in the early mornings, but it's not about that. \n\nAlthough, my husband has been watching this true crime documentary series on Netflix lately, and it's all about these cold cases from the seventies. It's so creepy. I don't know how he can watch that stuff right before bed. We actually had to upgrade our Netflix subscription because he wanted to watch it in 4K. He bought this massive new TV for the living room right before the Super Bowl. It's so big, we had to rearrange all the furniture just to make it fit. We ended up moving my favorite reading chair into the guest room, which is fine, I guess, because the lighting in there is actually really good in the afternoons. The guest room gets the western sun, so it warms up nicely. I have this beautiful spider plant hanging in the window in there, and it's just thriving. I tried growing succulents a few years ago because everyone said they were impossible to kill, but I somehow managed to drown them. I guess I overwatered them. My mother always had a green thumb, she could make anything grow. She had this huge vegetable garden in our backyard when I was growing up, and we'd eat fresh tomatoes all summer long. \n\nBut yeah, no... no thoughts about death or anything like that. Just the usual stress keeping me up.\n\nDoctor: Has your current state of feeling heavy and tired been consistent over the past month, or have you had any periods where you felt somewhat better?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been pretty consistent. Like, for about a month now, I've just been in this... this low place. Which is so weird because right before this, I was so energized and driven, getting everything done at work and at home. \n\nSpeaking of getting things done, I finally got around to organizing the garage last month when I had all that energy. We had so many boxes just sitting there since we moved in five years ago. My husband is a bit of a packrat, he keeps everything. He still has his old baseball cards from when he was a kid. He keeps them in these special plastic sleeves in a binder. I think he thinks they're going to be worth a fortune someday, but I looked up a few of them on eBay once and they were selling for like, two dollars. I buy a lot of stuff on eBay, actually. Mostly vintage Pyrex bowls. I love the primary colors, they just look so cheerful in the kitchen. I have this yellow mixing bowl that I use for everything, especially when I make my grandmother's banana bread recipe. She always used overripe bananas, the ones that are almost completely black. She said that's the secret to making it really sweet. She used to bake all the time, she made these incredible snickerdoodles for the church bake sale every year. The church we went to had this gorgeous stained glass window with a dove in the center. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the tiredness. It's just been a solid month of feeling really low and heavy, especially after having all that drive right before.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood stays with you all day, or are there times when you feel a little better?\n\nPatient: Well, it's pretty much there all day. Most days lately, I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like I'm already disappointed in the day before it even starts. It just kind of sits on me. \n\nWhich is frustrating because I used to love mornings. I used to get up early and make coffee in this French press I bought at a little boutique downtown. It's one of those shiny copper ones, really pretty. The shop I got it from is right next to this amazing bakery that makes the best almond croissants. They're huge, like the size of your hand, and covered in powdered sugar. I always end up getting it all over my clothes when I eat them. I have to carry a Tide pen in my purse now just in case. My purse is actually getting ridiculous, it's so heavy. I have like three different shades of lipstick in there, my planner, my wallet, and all these receipts I keep meaning to scan for work. I'm a manager, so I have to track all my expenses, and the new software they made us start using is just a nightmare. It takes ten steps just to upload one receipt. The IT guy, Kevin, tried to walk me through it on Zoom, but his microphone kept cutting out, so half the time he sounded like a robot. He has this huge collection of vintage action figures on the shelves behind his desk. I think they were Star Wars, but I couldn't really tell. My brother was obsessed with Star Wars when we were kids. He had the Millennium Falcon toy and he'd run around the house making the spaceship noises until my dad told him to knock it off. \n\nBut yeah, the low mood doesn't really lift. It's just this constant, heavy gray feeling from the minute I wake up until I go to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Is your low mood worse at any particular time of day?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed it being worse at a specific time, to be honest. It's just kind of... there. Always. \n\nAlthough, speaking of time of day, I've noticed the mail carrier has been coming a lot later in the afternoon recently. Usually, he's here by noon, but lately it's been closer to four o'clock. We have this really nice mail carrier, Frank. He always leaves a Milk-Bone for our neighbor's golden retriever. That dog is so sweet, his name is Buster. Buster actually got out of their yard last week and I found him wandering near the storm drain at the end of the street. It was right after that huge thunderstorm we had on Tuesday. The thunder was so loud it rattled the windows in the living room. We have these original single-pane windows from when the house was built in the fifties, and they are terrible for insulation. Our heating bill in the winter is just outrageous. I keep telling my husband we need to get them replaced, but he says it's not in the budget right now because of the new TV. He loves that TV. He was watching a documentary about the history of jazz the other night, and the sound quality was actually really impressive. I don't know much about jazz, but my grandfather used to play the saxophone. He had this beautiful brass saxophone that he kept polished to a shine. He played in a little band on the weekends at this local Italian restaurant. The restaurant made the most amazing garlic knots, they were always drowning in butter and parsley. \n\nBut no, I don't think the mood is worse at any particular time. It's just a steady heaviness all day long.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood can be lifted or made worse by things that happen during the day, like conversations with your family or events at work?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. It doesn't seem to matter what's going on around me. Like, even if I have a good conversation, the heaviness doesn't budge. \n\nActually, my sister called me just yesterday while I was on my lunch break. She's planning this huge family trip to Florida for next spring. She wants us all to rent this massive house near Disney World. I haven't been to Disney since I was a teenager. I remember it being so hot, just incredibly humid. We rode that Pirates of the Caribbean ride like four times just to sit in the air conditioning. My dad bought these ridiculous matching neon green t-shirts for all of us so we wouldn't get lost in the crowds. He was always so paranoid about losing one of us. He used to make us hold hands whenever we crossed the street, even when we were way too old for it. He was a safety inspector for the city, so I guess he just couldn't turn it off. He always had a hard hat and a clipboard in the back of his truck. It was this ancient Ford, manual transmission. I actually learned how to drive stick on that thing. Stalled it right in the middle of a busy intersection on my very first lesson, people were honking and I was just sweating through my shirt... \n\nBut, um, what were we saying? Oh, right. Yeah, even when she was telling me all about the vacation plans and getting all excited, I just couldn't feel it. Nothing really lifts it or makes it worse, it just stays exactly the same.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Like I was saying, it really is just consistently the same. I haven't noticed it fluctuating at all. 

It's actually a lot like the thermostat in my office building, now that I think about it. No matter what time of day it is, or what the weather is doing outside, it is just constantly freezing in there. I have to keep this thick wool cardigan draped over the back of my desk chair just to get through some of my afternoon meetings. It's this really heavy Irish cable-knit sweater my mother-in-law brought back for me from her trip to Dublin. She went over there with her church choir a couple of years ago, and they did this whole bus tour singing in all these beautiful old cathedrals. She brought back so many souvenirs, mostly these little ceramic sheep figurines. She has a whole glass display cabinet in her dining room just stuffed with them now. It sits right next to this antique grandfather clock she bought at an estate sale, and the thing chimes every fifteen minutes. It drives me absolutely crazy when we go over there for Thanksgiving. You can barely get through a sentence about the turkey before the clock starts bonging again. And her turkey is always so dry, too, she insists on roasting it without a foil tent, even though I've showed her how to do it a million times... 

But anyway, no, my mood doesn't really shift around. It's just that same flat, heavy feeling all day long.

Anyway, Bailey just shakes whenever the wind blows now. But me? No, no uncontrollable worry. Just... just so heavy and tired.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, usually I love painting, and I'm always the first one to want to go wandering through new museum exhibits. But recently... I just leave my supplies sitting there completely untouched, and I just can't make myself care enough to go anywhere. \n\nThere was actually this big modern art exhibit that opened downtown last week, and my friend Sarah got tickets for us months ago. Sarah is always so organized with things like that, she has this physical planner she carries everywhere with all these little stickers. I don't know how she keeps up with it all, honestly. She has three kids and they're all in travel soccer. Do you know how much driving travel soccer involves? They were in Ohio last weekend, and the weekend before that they had to drive all the way up to Pennsylvania. I get tired just hearing about it. I drive a Honda CRV and the seats are usually really comfortable for road trips—we took it to Maine a few years ago and it was great—but right now even just driving to the grocery store feels like a massive chore. \n\nBut anyway... yeah, I ended up telling Sarah I couldn't go to the museum. I just don't have any interest in my art or going out to see it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I haven't really been eating much. I've just been picking at my meals, or honestly, I'll completely forget to eat lunch at work. It's funny because my office is right next to this amazing little deli. They make these incredible turkey and cranberry sandwiches on sourdough. The sourdough comes from this bakery across town that's been run by the same family for like, three generations. The grandfather still comes in every morning at 4 AM to start the dough, which is just wild to me. I can't imagine getting up that early every single day. I used to be an early riser when I was in college, I'd get up and go for a run before my 8 AM classes. I went to school in upstate New York, so running in the winter was brutal. The snow would get so deep, sometimes they wouldn't even plow the side streets, and you'd just be trudging through it. My roommate, Jessica, she was from California and had never even seen snow before we moved in together. She bought this massive, puffy coat that made her look like a marshmallow, but she still froze. We lost touch after graduation, which is a shame. I think she moved to Chicago? Or maybe Seattle. But anyway, yeah, I've noticed my skirts are fitting a little looser lately, which is weird because I'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything. I just... I don't really have the appetite for it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your sleep pattern over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been really messed up lately. Even when I make a point to get into bed at a reasonable hour, I just... I wake up around four or five in the morning, and my brain just starts going. I start thinking about work, and things with my husband, and then I'm just done sleeping. I can't get back to sleep at all. \n\nIt's actually really frustrating because our mattress is supposed to be one of those super comfortable memory foam ones. We bought it last year during a Presidents' Day sale. You know how those furniture stores always have those crazy holiday sales with the giant inflatable gorillas out front? There's this one store on the highway near our house that has this guy who stands out there spinning a sign, no matter what the weather is. I always feel so bad for him when it's raining. I mean, they must pay him decently, right? Or maybe it's just a high school kid looking for some extra cash. My nephew, Ryan, just started his first job at a movie theater. He gets free popcorn, which sounds amazing, but he says the smell of butter gets stuck in his clothes and he can't wash it out. He's saving up for a car. He really wants a Jeep, but my sister is terrified of him driving one because they can roll over. She's always been a bit of a worrier, ever since we were kids. \n\nBut yeah, I just... I lie there at 4 AM, wide awake, and I just can't turn my brain off to go back to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Well, actually, yeah. My staff at work has definitely noticed it. I'm a manager, so I run a lot of meetings, and lately they've been pointing out that I'm just... slower to answer questions. Like there's this long pause before I can even get my thoughts together and get the words out. \n\nIt's really embarrassing, honestly, especially in front of the newer hires. We just brought on this young guy, Michael, straight out of college. He went to Penn State, which is where my cousin's daughter is looking to go next year. She wants to study marine biology, which I think is just fascinating. I took a marine biology elective once and we got to go on this field trip to an aquarium and actually touch the stingrays. They feel so weird, kind of slimy but smooth at the same time. The aquarium was down in Baltimore, right on the Inner Harbor. Have you ever been there? Vegas is just too much for me, honestly. All the flashing lights and the noise, it gives me a headache. \n\nBut anyway, yeah... the meetings. It's just taking me longer to process things and respond to people. He's got this goofy smile and one floppy ear that never stands up straight. We used to go apple picking out there every fall when we were kids. My mom would make these massive batches of homemade applesauce and can them in those glass Mason jars. She had this whole system with boiling water and tongs, and she'd always make us label them with these little sticky tags she bought at the craft store. The craft store closed down a few years ago, which is sad. It got replaced by one of those giant chain pharmacies. Just feeling a little off my game, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death recurring in your mind, even if you don't want to die?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really been thinking about death. He bought this massive new TV for the living room right before the Super Bowl. It's so big, we had to rearrange all the furniture just to make it fit. We ended up moving my favorite reading chair into the guest room, which is fine, I guess, because the lighting in there is actually really good in the afternoons. The guest room gets the western sun, so it warms up nicely. I have this beautiful spider plant hanging in the window in there, and it's just thriving. I tried growing succulents a few years ago because everyone said they were impossible to kill, but I somehow managed to drown them. I guess I overwatered them. My mother always had a green thumb, she could make anything grow. She had this huge vegetable garden in our backyard when I was growing up, and we'd eat fresh tomatoes all summer long. \n\nBut yeah, no... no thoughts about death or anything like that. My dad bought these ridiculous matching neon green t-shirts for all of us so we wouldn't get lost in the crowds. He was always so paranoid about losing one of us. He used to make us hold hands whenever we crossed the street, even when we were way too old for it. He was a safety inspector for the city, so I guess he just couldn't turn it off. Stalled it right in the middle of a busy intersection on my very first lesson, people were honking and I was just sweating through my shirt... \n\nBut, um, what were we saying? Oh, right. Yeah, even when she was telling me all about the vacation plans and getting all excited, I just couldn't feel it. Nothing really lifts it or makes it worse, it just stays exactly the same.\n\nDoctor: Does your low mood ever feel slightly better or worse at any point during the day, or is it consistently the same?\n\nPatient: Like I was saying, it really is just consistently the same. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I'd say it's... mild, maybe? I mean, I'm still showing up for work and doing what I need to do around the house. I haven't completely stopped functioning or anything. 

Though, I have been a lot slower making decisions at work lately. It's like my brain is just wading through molasses. We had this big vendor contract to renew last week, and I just stared at the spreadsheets for days without signing off. The vendor is this local printing company, they do all our brochures and training manuals. The guy who runs it, Tom, he actually used to be a minor league baseball player before he blew out his knee. He has all this memorabilia in his office, signed bats and jerseys framed on the walls. I don't really follow baseball, but my nephew is obsessed. He plays Little League, and he's a pitcher. He actually pitched a no-hitter last season, and my brother was so proud he bought the whole team pizza afterwards. They went to this arcade pizza place down by the highway, the kind with the animatronic band that plays those creepy songs. I always hated those things when I was a kid, the eyes on the robot bear always looked like they were following me. It's funny how things like that stick with you, isn't it? Like, I can still remember the exact smell of the carpet in that arcade, just a mix of stale pepperoni and floor wax...

But yeah, I'm still functioning. I've just been less reliable with family plans, and I've totally withdrawn from my art activities. I usually paint watercolors on the weekends, it keeps me connected, but I just haven't had the energy for it. So I guess it's impacting me, but I'm still getting by.

I think it's more the second one, honestly. I'm just feeling really low and I can't seem to find pleasure in much of anything. 

Like I was saying about my watercolors... I usually love painting. And I normally spend a lot of my weekends just wandering through museum exhibits, taking it all in. But recently, I just leave my supplies completely untouched. I can’t even make myself care enough to go to a museum. The last time I went was to see this impressionist exhibit downtown. They had a few Monets, which are always beautiful, but I actually prefer Degas. His pastels of the ballet dancers are just incredible. I actually took ballet when I was a little girl, for about six years. I was never very good at it, I was always a bit clumsy. My instructor, Madame Dubois, she was this very strict French woman who always wore her hair in this incredibly tight bun. I swear it looked like it was pulling her eyebrows up. She used to tap her cane on the wooden floor to keep time, and the sound of it echoing in that big studio used to make me so nervous. The studio was above a bakery, and the smell of fresh bread and croissants would waft up through the floorboards. It was the best smell. My mom would always buy me a chocolate croissant after class if I didn't complain too much about my feet hurting. She'd get a coffee, and we'd sit on this little green bench outside... 

But, um, anyway. No, I'm just not enjoying things right now. I just can't seem to muster up the interest.

He bought this massive new TV for the living room right before the Super Bowl. It's so big, we had to rearrange all the furniture just to make it fit. My mother always had a green thumb, she could make anything grow. Mostly vintage Pyrex bowls. I love the primary colors, they just look so cheerful in the kitchen. I have this yellow mixing bowl that I use for everything, especially when I make my grandmother's banana bread recipe. She always used overripe bananas, the ones that are almost completely black. She said that's the secret to making it really sweet. She used to bake all the time, she made these incredible snickerdoodles for the church bake sale every year. The church we went to had this gorgeous stained glass window with a dove in the center. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the tiredness. It's just been a solid month of feeling really low and heavy, especially after having all that drive right before.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood stays with you all day, or are there times when you feel a little better?\n\nPatient: Well, it's pretty much there all day. Most days lately, I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like I'm already disappointed in the day before it even starts. It just kind of sits on me. \n\nWhich is frustrating because I used to love mornings. I used to get up early and make coffee in this French press I bought at a little boutique downtown. It's one of those shiny copper ones, really pretty. The shop I got it from is right next to this amazing bakery that makes the best almond croissants. They're huge, like the size of your hand, and covered in powdered sugar. I always end up getting it all over my clothes when I eat them. I have to carry a Tide pen in my purse now just in case. My purse is actually getting ridiculous, it's so heavy. I have like three different shades of lipstick in there, my planner, my wallet, and all these receipts I keep meaning to scan for work. I'm a manager, so I have to track all my expenses, and the new software they made us start using is just a nightmare. It takes ten steps just to upload one receipt. The IT guy, Kevin, tried to walk me through it on Zoom, but his microphone kept cutting out, so half the time he sounded like a robot. He has this huge collection of vintage action figures on the shelves behind his desk. I think they were Star Wars, but I couldn't really tell. My brother was obsessed with Star Wars when we were kids. He had the Millennium Falcon toy and he'd run around the house making the spaceship noises until my dad told him to knock it off. \n\nBut yeah, the low mood doesn't really lift. It's just this constant, heavy gray feeling from the minute I wake up until I go to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Is your low mood worse at any particular time of day?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed it being worse at a specific time, to be honest. It's just kind of... there. Always. \n\nAlthough, speaking of time of day, I've noticed the mail carrier has been coming a lot later in the afternoon recently. Usually, he's here by noon, but lately it's been closer to four o'clock. We have this really nice mail carrier, Frank. He always leaves a Milk-Bone for our neighbor's golden retriever. That dog is so sweet, his name is Buster. Buster actually got out of their yard last week and I found him wandering near the storm drain at the end of the street. It was right after that huge thunderstorm we had on Tuesday. The thunder was so loud it rattled the windows in the living room. We have these original single-pane windows from when the house was built in the fifties, and they are terrible for insulation. Our heating bill in the winter is just outrageous. I keep telling my husband we need to get them replaced, but he says it's not in the budget right now because of the new TV. He loves that TV. He was watching a documentary about the history of jazz the other night, and the sound quality was actually really impressive. I don't know much about jazz, but my grandfather used to play the saxophone. He had this beautiful brass saxophone that he kept polished to a shine. He played in a little band on the weekends at this local Italian restaurant. The restaurant made the most amazing garlic knots, they were always drowning in butter and parsley. \n\nBut no, I don't think the mood is worse at any particular time. It's just a steady heaviness all day long.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood can be lifted or made worse by things that happen during the day, like conversations with your family or events at work?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. It doesn't seem to matter what's going on around me. Like, even if I have a good conversation, the heaviness doesn't budge. \n\nActually, my sister called me just yesterday while I was on my lunch break. She's planning this huge family trip to Florida for next spring. She wants us all to rent this massive house near Disney World. I haven't been to Disney since I was a teenager. I remember it being so hot, just incredibly humid. We rode that Pirates of the Caribbean ride like four times just to sit in the air conditioning. My dad bought these ridiculous matching neon green t-shirts for all of us so we wouldn't get lost in the crowds. He was always so paranoid about losing one of us. He used to make us hold hands whenever we crossed the street, even when we were way too old for it. He was a safety inspector for the city, so I guess he just couldn't turn it off. He always had a hard hat and a clipboard in the back of his truck. It was this ancient Ford, manual transmission. I actually learned how to drive stick on that thing. Stalled it right in the middle of a busy intersection on my very first lesson, people were honking and I was just sweating through my shirt... \n\nBut, um, what were we saying? Oh, right. Yeah, even when she was telling me all about the vacation plans and getting all excited, I just couldn't feel it. Nothing really lifts it or makes it worse, it just stays exactly the same.\n\nDoctor: Does your low mood ever feel slightly better or worse at any point during the day, or is it consistently the same?\n\nPatient: Like I was saying, it really is just consistently the same. I haven't noticed it fluctuating at all. \n\nIt's actually a lot like the thermostat in my office building, now that I think about it. No matter what time of day it is, or what the weather is doing outside, it is just constantly freezing in there. I have to keep this thick wool cardigan draped over the back of my desk chair just to get through some of my afternoon meetings. It's this really heavy Irish cable-knit sweater my mother-in-law brought back for me from her trip to Dublin. She went over there with her church choir a couple of years ago, and they did this whole bus tour singing in all these beautiful old cathedrals. She brought back so many souvenirs, mostly these little ceramic sheep figurines. She has a whole glass display cabinet in her dining room just stuffed with them now. It sits right next to this antique grandfather clock she bought at an estate sale, and the thing chimes every fifteen minutes. It drives me absolutely crazy when we go over there for Thanksgiving. You can barely get through a sentence about the turkey before the clock starts bonging again. And her turkey is always so dry, too, she insists on roasting it without a foil tent, even though I've showed her how to do it a million times... \n\nBut anyway, no, my mood doesn't really shift around. It's just that same flat, heavy feeling all day long.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood is affecting your daily life in a mild way, or is it having a more significant impact on your ability to function?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's... mild, maybe? I mean, I'm still showing up for work and doing what I need to do around the house. I haven't completely stopped functioning or anything. \n\nThough, I have been a lot slower making decisions at work lately. It's like my brain is just wading through molasses. We had this big vendor contract to renew last week, and I just stared at the spreadsheets for days without signing off. The vendor is this local printing company, they do all our brochures and training manuals. The guy who runs it, Tom, he actually used to be a minor league baseball player before he blew out his knee. He has all this memorabilia in his office, signed bats and jerseys framed on the walls. I don't really follow baseball, but my nephew is obsessed. He plays Little League, and he's a pitcher. He actually pitched a no-hitter last season, and my brother was so proud he bought the whole team pizza afterwards. They went to this arcade pizza place down by the highway, the kind with the animatronic band that plays those creepy songs. I always hated those things when I was a kid, the eyes on the robot bear always looked like they were following me. It's funny how things like that stick with you, isn't it? Like, I can still remember the exact smell of the carpet in that arcade, just a mix of stale pepperoni and floor wax...\n\nBut yeah, I'm still functioning. I've just been less reliable with family plans, and I've totally withdrawn from my art activities. I usually paint watercolors on the weekends, it keeps me connected, but I just haven't had the energy for it. So I guess it's impacting me, but I'm still getting by.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current state as feeling somewhat down but still able to enjoy some things, or is it more that you're persistently feeling very low and unable to find pleasure in almost anything?\n\nPatient: I think it's more the second one, honestly. I'm just feeling really low and I can't seem to find pleasure in much of anything. \n\nLike I was saying about my watercolors... I usually love painting. And I normally spend a lot of my weekends just wandering through museum exhibits, taking it all in. But recently, I just leave my supplies completely untouched. I can’t even make myself care enough to go to a museum. The last time I went was to see this impressionist exhibit downtown. They had a few Monets, which are always beautiful, but I actually prefer Degas. His pastels of the ballet dancers are just incredible. I actually took ballet when I was a little girl, for about six years. I was never very good at it, I was always a bit clumsy. My instructor, Madame Dubois, she was this very strict French woman who always wore her hair in this incredibly tight bun. I swear it looked like it was pulling her eyebrows up. She used to tap her cane on the wooden floor to keep time, and the sound of it echoing in that big studio used to make me so nervous. The studio was above a bakery, and the smell of fresh bread and croissants would waft up through the floorboards. It was the best smell. My mom would always buy me a chocolate croissant after class if I didn't complain too much about my feet hurting. She'd get a coffee, and we'd sit on this little green bench outside... \n\nBut, um, anyway. No, I'm just not enjoying things right now. I just can't seem to muster up the interest."
  }
